Proof.

Recall the operator definition:

A_{l}\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})=-|D|(1+e^{-2|D|})^{-1}\frac{1}{2\pi}\Big(\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})+\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})+\tilde{R}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})\Big),

where the components are defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}}\cdot\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\tilde{p}_{l}(P)\xi(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}},

\displaystyle l\text{ odd},

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4}\cdot\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}Q\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\xi(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}},

\displaystyle l\geq 1,

and

\tilde{R}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{4Q}{(|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{2}}\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})+\frac{2}{(|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3/2}}\tilde{q}_{l}(Q,\kappa_{h})\right)\xi(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}.

Case 1:
l=1
.

The operator simplifies to

A_{1}(\eta)\xi=D\eta\cdot D\xi-|D|(1+e^{-2|D|})^{-1}\eta(1+e^{-2|D|})G_{0}\xi,

where
G_{0}=|D|\tanh(|D|)
. This implies

\displaystyle\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta)(\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi)\|_{s,q}+C\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}(\eta(1+e^{-2|D|})G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+C\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|(1+e^{-2|D|})G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|(1+e^{-2|D|})\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}.

Using the kernel
M_{2}(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})=\frac{1}{\pi(|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3/2}}
and boundedness of
\tanh(|D|)
in
W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we obtain

\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}.

Case 2:
l\geq 2
, odd.

Analyze
\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=-l\tilde{a}_{l}\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{3}}\cdot P^{l-1}\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\xi(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+l\tilde{a}_{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{3}}\cdot P^{l-1}\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\eta\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\xi\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad-\tilde{a}_{l}\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{3}}\cdot P^{l-1}\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\xi(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{a}_{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{3}}\cdot P^{l-1}\eta(\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\xi\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}.

By Lemma
3.1
, this yields

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq Cl^{M+s+1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{l-2}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big).

Case 3:
\tilde{Q}_{l,*}
for
l\geq 2
.

Rewrite the operator using the substitution
\mathbf{y^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}{(|\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3/2}}\cdot\partial_{\mathbf{y^{\prime}}}\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}}{(|\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{5/2}}(\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})+\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}}))\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\xi(\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}})\,d\mathbf{y^{\prime}},

with
Q=\frac{\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})+\eta(\mathbf{x^{\prime}}-\mathbf{y^{\prime}})}{(|\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{1/2}}
,
\kappa_{h}=\frac{4}{(|\mathbf{y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{1/2}}
and
\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})=\sum_{j=0}^{[(l-1)/2]}\tilde{b}_{l-1-j}\kappa_{h}^{l-1-2j}Q^{l-1}
. Differentiation gives

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x^{\prime}}}\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi({\bf x^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{{\bf y^{\prime}}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3/2}}\cdot\partial_{{\bf y^{\prime}}}^{2}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{[(l-1)/2]}\tilde{b}_{l-1-j}(l-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{{\bf y^{\prime}}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{2}}\cdot\partial_{{\bf y^{\prime}}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})\kappa_{h}^{l-1-2j}Q^{l-2}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}})-\partial_{{\bf y^{\prime}}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}}))\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{{\bf y^{\prime}}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3/2}}\cdot\partial_{{\bf y^{\prime}}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\partial_{\bf y^{\prime}}\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{5/2}}(\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}})-\partial_{\bf y^{\prime}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}}))\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{[(l-1)/2]}\tilde{b}_{l-1-j}(l-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{3}}(\eta({\bf x^{\prime}})+\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}}))\kappa_{h}^{l-1-2j}Q^{l-2}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}})-\partial_{\bf y^{\prime}}\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}}))\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}}{(|{\bf y^{\prime}}|^{2}+4)^{5/2}}(\eta({\bf x^{\prime}})+\eta({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}}))\tilde{q}_{l-1}(Q,\kappa_{h})\partial_{\bf y^{\prime}}\xi({\bf x^{\prime}}-{\bf y^{\prime}})d{\bf y^{\prime}}.

By Lemma
3.2
, for
l=2
:

\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{Q}_{2,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

and for
l\geq 3
:

\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq Cl^{s+1}2^{l}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}^{l-3}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\Big).

Case 4:
\tilde{R}_{l,*}
for
l\geq 2
.

After differentiation and simplification:

\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{R}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq Cl2^{l}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}^{l-2}\|\eta\|_{C^{s}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\Big).

Final estimates:

•

Operator
A_{2}
:

\displaystyle\|A_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{Q}_{2,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{R}_{2,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big).

•

Operator
A_{l}
(
l\geq 3
):

\displaystyle\|A_{l}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{P}_{l}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{Q}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\tilde{R}_{l,*}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Cl^{M+s+1}2^{l}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{l-3}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big).

•

Operator
A_{L}^{R}
:

\displaystyle\|A_{L}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=L+1}^{\infty}\|A_{j}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq CL^{M+s+1}2^{L}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{L-3}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big).

This completes the proof.
∎

Moreover, the proof of the preceding theorem yields the following uniform continuity estimate for the operator
A_{l}
:

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
. The operators
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{l}
, and
A_{L}^{R}
satisfy the estimates:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}A_{1}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}A_{2}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

and for all integers
l,L\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}A_{l}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq Cl^{M+s+2}2^{l}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{1}}^{l-3}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}A_{L}^{R}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq CL^{M+s+2}2^{L}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{1}}^{L-3}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

where
\eta_{t}=\eta_{1}+t(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})
for
t\in[0,1]
, and
C>0
is a constant.

Proof.

As the derivation follows similar arguments to the proof of Theorem
3.4
, we present the detailed calculation only for
A_{1}
. The estimates for the remaining operators
A_{2}
,
A_{l}
(
l\geq 3
), and
A_{L}^{R}
(
L\geq 3
) follow analogously. From the operator expression:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}A_{1}(\eta_{t})\xi=D(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\cdot D\xi-|D|(1+e^{-2|D|})^{-1}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})(1+e^{-2|D|})G_{0}\xi,

we obtain the norm estimate:

\left\|\frac{d}{dt}A_{1}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big).

∎

Remark 3.3
.

Existing estimates for
A_{l}
and
A_{L}^{R}
in
[
21
,
62
]
state that for
1<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\|A_{l}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{l-1}\Big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q}\Big),

and

\|A_{L}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{L-1}\Big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q}\Big).

Theorem
3.4
improves these bounds by establishing sharper norm estimates, which provide enhanced control over the remainder term
R_{3}
in subsequent error analyses.

3.3.
Estimate of the remainder operators
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
, and
R_{3}

This subsection establishes bounds for the remainder terms in the Dirichlet-Neumann operator expansion, extending Craig’s results. We focus particularly on the Taylor remainder
R_{3}
.

Theorem 3.5
.

Let
1<q<\infty
satisfy
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
with
s\geq 0
. Suppose
\eta
satisfies

\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{4(1+C_{0})},\quad\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}<\infty.

Then the Taylor remainder
R_{3}(\eta)
from the expansion of the Dirichlet-Neumann operator
G(\eta)
satisfies

\big\||D|^{-1}R_{3}(\eta)\xi\big\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}},

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\eta
and
\xi
.

Proof.

We recall that

\begin{split}R_{3}&=\big(B_{3}^{R}+B_{1}(B_{2}+B_{3}^{R})+B_{2}(B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3}^{R})+B_{3}^{R}(B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3}^{R})\big)G_{0}\\
&\quad+\big(B_{2}+B_{3}^{R}\big)A_{1}+(1-B)^{-1}\big(A_{3}^{R}+BA_{2}+B^{2}A_{1}+B^{3}G_{0}\big)\\
&=\big(B_{3}^{R}+B_{1}B_{2}^{R}+B_{2}^{R}B_{1}^{R}\big)G_{0}+B_{2}^{R}A_{1}\\
&\quad+(1-B)^{-1}\big(A_{3}^{R}+B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\big).\end{split}

By Remark
3.2
and Theorem
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}B_{3}^{R}(\eta)(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}},

\||D|^{-1}B_{1}(\eta)B_{2}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}},

and

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}B_{2}^{R}(\eta)B_{1}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|B_{1}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|B_{1}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}.

Using Remark
3.3
and Theorem
3.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}B_{2}^{R}(\eta)A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big).

From Lemma
3.3
, the Neumann series

|D|^{-1}(1-B)^{-1}A_{3}^{R}=|D|^{-1}A_{3}^{R}+|D|^{-1}B\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}

converges for
\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<1/(1+C_{0})
, since the geometric series
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+C_{0})^{m-1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m-1}
and
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m}
converge in this region. By Theorems
3.3
and
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}(1-B(\eta))^{-1}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\||D|^{-1}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\Big\||D|^{-1}B\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\Big\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\Big(\Big\|\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\Big\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\Big\|\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\Big\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big\|\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\Big\|_{q_{2}}\Big).

Lemma
3.3
and Remark
3.3
yield

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\Big\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\|B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C(1+C_{0})^{m-1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}(1-B(\eta))^{-1}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{3}\big(\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big).

Similarly, Lemma
3.3
implies the Neumann series

\displaystyle|D|^{-1}(1-B)^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})

\displaystyle=|D|^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})+|D|^{-1}B\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})

converges for
\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<1/(1+C_{0})
. Theorems
3.3
and
3.1
give

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}(1-B)^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\||D|^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}+\Big\||D|^{-1}B\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\Big\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\Big(\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\Big\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\Big\|_{q_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\Big(\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\Big\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\Big\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\Big\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\Big\|_{q_{2}}\Big).

Remark
3.3
and Lemma
3.3
imply

\displaystyle\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big),

and

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\Big\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+C_{0})^{m-1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big).

Hence,

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}(1-B)^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\big(\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big).

Combining all estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\big(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{3}\big(\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\big(\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Using estimates similar to those in the previous theorem, we obtain the following uniform continuity result for the remainder term
|D|^{-1}R_{3}
.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
1<q<\infty
with
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
. Suppose
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
satisfy

\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{4(1+C_{0})},\quad\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{s+1}}<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta_{i}\|_{s,q_{1}}<\infty

for
i=1,2
. Then for the Taylor remainder
R_{3}(\eta)
in the expansion of the Dirichlet-Neumann operator
G(\eta)
, we have

\left\|\frac{d}{dt}|D|^{-1}R_{3}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}

where
\eta_{t}=(1-t)\eta_{1}+t\eta_{2}
and
t\in[0,1]
, with
C>0
independent of
\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\xi
, and
t
.

Proof.

Using the explicit form of the
R_{3}
operator, this result is established by applying
Corollaries
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3
through the same analytical
framework developed in the preceding theorem’s proof.
∎

We need to make a slight modification to the norm of
R_{3}
to align with our nonlinear term estimates in Section
5
.

Theorem 3.6
.

Let
1<q<\infty
with
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
. Assume
\eta
satisfies

\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{4(1+C_{0})},\quad\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}<\infty.

Then the remainder term
R_{3}(\eta)
satisfies the estimate:

\|R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right)

where
C>0
is independent of
\eta
and
\xi
.

Proof.

Recall the expression for
R_{3}
:

R_{3}=\big(B_{3}^{R}+B_{1}B_{2}^{R}+B_{2}^{R}B_{1}^{R}\big)G_{0}+B_{2}^{R}A_{1}+(1-B)^{-1}\big(A_{3}^{R}+B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\big).

Applying Theorem
3.2
, we estimate the first term:

\displaystyle\|B_{3}^{R}(\eta)(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right).

For the second term, combining Remark
3.2
and Theorem
3.2
yields

\displaystyle\|B_{1}(\eta)B_{2}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|B_{2}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|B_{2}^{R}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\big(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{q_{1}}\|G_{0}\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}G_{0}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\big(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\big(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\big).

For the third term, since
B_{1}^{R}=B_{2}+B_{2}^{R}
, Remark
3.2
gives

\displaystyle\|B_{2}^{R}B_{1}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|B_{1}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|B_{1}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}\right).

Furthermore, Theorem
3.2
implies

\displaystyle\|B_{1}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\|B_{2}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}+\|B_{2}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right).

Combining these yields

\displaystyle\|B_{2}^{R}B_{1}^{R}(G_{0}\xi)\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{2}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right).

Next, using Remark
3.2
and Theorem
3.4
, we estimate
B_{2}^{R}A_{1}
:

\displaystyle\|B_{2}^{R}(\eta)A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\big(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\big).

We now turn to the terms involving
(1-B)^{-1}
. From the estimate on
B^{m}(\eta)
in Lemma
3.3
, the Neumann series

(1-B)^{-1}A_{3}^{R}=A_{3}^{R}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}

converges absolutely for
\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<1/(1+C_{0})
, as the series
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+C_{0})^{m-1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m-1}
and
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m}
converge within this radius. Applying Lemma
3.3
and Theorem
3.4
gives

\displaystyle\|(1-B(\eta))^{-1}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\left\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}B^{m}A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+C_{0})^{m-1}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}^{m-1}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{3}^{R}(\eta)\xi\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right).

Similarly, for the remaining part of the inverse term, Lemma
3.3
and Theorem
3.4
imply

\displaystyle\|(1-B)^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}+C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\left(\|A_{2}\xi\|_{s,q}+\|BA_{1}\xi\|_{s,q}+\|B^{2}G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|A_{2}\xi\|_{q}+\|BA_{1}\xi\|_{q}+\|B^{2}G_{0}\xi\|_{q}\right),

where the individual estimates are

\displaystyle\|A_{2}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|BA_{1}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{1}\xi\|_{q}+\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|A_{1}\xi\|_{s,q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right),

and, using Lemma
3.3
and Theorem
3.2
,

\displaystyle\|B^{2}G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|BG_{0}\xi\|_{q}+C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|BG_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}(1+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}})\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\right).

Combining these estimates leads to

\displaystyle\|(1-B)^{-1}B(A_{2}+BA_{1}+B^{2}G_{0})\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right).

Summing up all the estimates for the constituent parts of
R_{3}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\bf x^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right),

completing the proof.
∎

Regarding the equi-continuity property of
R_{3}
, we establish the following result.

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
1<q<+\infty
with
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
.
Suppose
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
satisfy

\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{4(1+C_{0})},\quad\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{s+1}}<+\infty,\quad\|\eta_{i}\|_{s,q_{1}}<+\infty,\quad i=1,2.

Then for
\eta_{t}=\eta_{1}+t(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})
with
t\in[0,1]
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}R_{3}(\eta_{t})\xi\right\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta_{t}\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
q,s
.

Proof.

Combining Corollaries
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3
with the explicit expression for the
R_{3}
operator,
we complete the proof through direct application of the methodology established above.
∎

Given the norm estimate for
R_{3}
and the relation
R_{2}=G_{2}+R_{3}
, we derive the following boundedness result for
|D|^{-1}R_{2}
.

Theorem 3.7
.

Let
1<q<+\infty
with
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
.
Assume
\eta
satisfies

\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{1+C_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}<+\infty.

Then the Taylor remainder
R_{2}(\eta)
in the expansion of
G(\eta)
admits the estimate:

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}R_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
q,s
.

Proof.

Starting from the decomposition

R_{2}(\eta)\xi=G_{2}(\eta)\xi+R_{3}(\eta)\xi

with

G_{2}(\eta)=B_{2}G_{0}+B_{1}A_{1}+A_{2}+B_{1}^{2}G_{0},

we estimate each component separately.
For
|D|^{-1}B_{2}G_{0}
, Theorem
3.1
yields

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}B_{2}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|G_{0}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right).

For
|D|^{-1}B_{1}A_{1}
, first apply Theorem
3.4
:

\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{q_{2}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}B_{1}(\eta)A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{q_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\left(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right).

For
|D|^{-1}A_{2}
, Theorem
3.3
gives

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}A_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\right).

For
|D|^{-1}B_{1}^{2}G_{0}
, combine Theorem
3.1
and Remark
3.2
:

\||D|^{-1}B_{1}^{2}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|B_{1}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\leq C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}.

For the remainder term, Theorem
3.5
provides

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}.

Combining these estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}R_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\||D|^{-1}G_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\||D|^{-1}R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\left(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}.

Since
\|\xi\|_{1,q_{2}}\leq\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}
, we can consolidate terms to achieve the final estimate:

\displaystyle\||D|^{-1}R_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{\max\{s,1\},q_{1}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{q_{2}},

completing the proof.
∎

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
1<q<\infty
satisfy
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}
, and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq 0
.
Suppose
\eta
satisfies

\|\eta\|_{C^{1}}<\frac{1}{1+C_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}<\infty.

Then the remainder term
R_{2}(\eta)
admits the estimate:

\displaystyle\|R_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C(1+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}})\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

where
C>0
is a constant depending on
q,s
.

Proof.

Starting from the decomposition

R_{2}(\eta)\xi=G_{2}(\eta)\xi+R_{3}(\eta)\xi

with

G_{2}(\eta)=B_{2}G_{0}+B_{1}A_{1}+A_{2}+B_{1}^{2}G_{0},

we establish estimates for each component.

Estimate for
B_{2}G_{0}\xi
:
By Theorem
3.2
,

\displaystyle\|B_{2}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big).

Estimate for
B_{1}A_{1}\xi
:
Combining Remark
3.2
and Theorem
3.4
yields

\displaystyle\|B_{1}(\eta)A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|A_{1}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big).

Estimate for
A_{2}\xi
:
Applying Theorem
3.4
gives

\displaystyle\|A_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big).

Estimate for
B_{1}^{2}G_{0}\xi
:
Using Remark
3.2
and Theorem
3.2
,

\displaystyle\|B_{1}^{2}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\|B_{1}(\eta)G_{0}\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\xi\|_{s,q_{2}}\Big).

Estimate for
R_{3}\xi
:
Theorem
3.6
provides

\displaystyle\|R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big).

Summing these estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\|R_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq\|G_{2}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}+\|R_{3}(\eta)\xi\|_{s,q}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}})\|\eta\|_{C^{s+1}}\Big(\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}+\|\eta\|_{s+1,q_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\xi\|_{s+1,q_{2}}\Big),

which completes the proof.
∎

4.
The modified linearized KP-I operator

This section investigates the mapping properties of the modified linearized KP-I operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}
. Specifically, we analyze solutions to the equation:

(4.1)

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\phi=\partial_{1}h_{1}+\partial_{2}h_{2},

where the operator is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{L}_{1}+\mathcal{L}_{2}-\frac{3}{c}\partial_{1}(\partial_{1}q\partial_{1}(\cdot)).

The functions
\phi
,
h_{1}
, and
h_{2}
belong to the following function spaces:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}:\|f\|_{a}<\infty\right\},\quad\mathcal{H}_{2}:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{e}}:\|f\|_{b}<\infty\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}:\|f\|_{c}<\infty\right\},

equipped with the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{a}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}f|^{2}+|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}f|^{2}+|\nabla^{3}_{\varepsilon}f|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}f|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}\right)dx\,dy,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{b}

\displaystyle:=\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\partial_{1}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{c}

\displaystyle:=\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\partial_{2}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
\nabla_{\varepsilon}:=(\partial_{1},\varepsilon\partial_{2})
. The symmetry classes are defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f:f(x,y)=-f(-x,y)=f(x,-y)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f:f(x,y)=f(-x,y)=f(x,-y)\right\}.

The first result establishes an a-priori estimate for the linearized operator.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{1}
solve (
4.1
) with
h_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{3}
. Then there exist constants
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,

(4.2)

\|\phi\|_{a}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{b}+\|h_{2}\|_{c}\right),

where
C
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

Our proof follows the strategy in
[
47
]
, adapted to our context. We present the key steps for completeness.

Step 1: Functional setting and spectral decomposition.

Define the space
\mathcal{E}
as the closure of
\partial_{1}(C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
under the norm

\|\partial_{1}f\|_{\mathcal{E}}:=\left(\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\|\partial_{1}^{2}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The antiderivative
\partial_{1}^{-1}
is well-defined on
\mathcal{E}
(cf.
[
6
, Remark 1.1]
). Consider the self-adjoint operator in
\mathcal{E}
:

L\phi:=A\partial_{1}^{2}\phi-\phi-\frac{3}{c}(\partial_{1}q\phi)-(1+\varepsilon^{2})\partial_{1}^{-2}\partial_{2}^{2}\phi.

By
[
46
, Theorem 2]
,
L
has a unique negative eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}
with associated even eigenfunction
\phi_{0}
satisfying:

A\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{0}-\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{c}(\partial_{1}q_{\varepsilon}\phi_{0})-(1+\varepsilon^{2})\partial_{1}^{-2}\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{0}+\lambda_{1}\phi_{0}=0.

Moreover,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{0}(x,y)dx=0
for each
y
, and
\phi_{0}
decays at infinity. Define:

\phi_{1}:=\partial_{1}^{-1}\phi_{0}=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\phi_{0}(z,y)dz,\quad\partial_{1}^{-2}\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{0}:=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\int_{-\infty}^{z}\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{0}(w,y)dwdz.

Then
\phi_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}
and satisfies

A\partial_{1}^{4}\phi_{1}-\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}-\frac{3}{c}\partial_{1}(\partial_{1}q\partial_{1}\phi_{1})-(1+\varepsilon^{2})\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{1}=-\lambda_{1}\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}.

As
\varepsilon\to 0
,
\lambda_{1}
converges to the unique negative eigenvalue of the linearized equation for (
2.12
).

Step 2: Orthogonal decomposition.

Decompose
\phi=a\phi_{1}+\phi_{2}
with

a=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{1}\phi\partial_{1}\phi_{1}dxdy}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{1}\phi_{1})^{2}dxdy},

where
\phi_{2}
belongs to the orthogonal complement of
\phi_{1}
in
\mathcal{H}_{1}
under the inner product:

\displaystyle(f,g)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big[A\partial_{1}^{2}f\partial_{1}^{2}g+\partial_{1}f\partial_{1}g+\frac{3}{c}\partial_{1}q\partial_{1}f\partial_{1}g+(1+\varepsilon^{2})\partial_{2}f\partial_{2}g\Big]dxdy.

For any
\psi
in this complement, we have

(\psi,\psi)\geq\lambda_{2}\|\partial_{1}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2},

where
\lambda_{2}>0
is the smallest positive eigenvalue of
L
, uniformly bounded for small
\varepsilon
. Additionally,

(4.3)

(\psi,\psi)\geq\lambda_{*}\left(\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+A\|\partial_{1}^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\right),

with
\lambda_{*}\in(0,1)
independent of
\varepsilon
.

Step 3: Equation for
\phi_{2}
and key estimates.

Equation (
4.1
) becomes

(4.4)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\phi_{2}&=a\lambda_{1}\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}-a\left[\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{1}^{2}\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{1}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{2}^{4}\phi_{1}+\mathcal{L}_{2}\phi_{1}\right]\\
&\quad+\partial_{1}h_{1}+\partial_{2}h_{2}.\end{split}

Multiply by
a\phi_{1}
and integrate by parts

(4.5)

\begin{split}&a\left[\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\langle\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{2},\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\rangle+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}\langle\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{2},\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{1}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}\phi_{2},\phi_{1}\rangle\right]\\
&=-a^{2}\left[\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}\phi_{1},\phi_{1}\rangle\right]\\
&\quad-a^{2}\lambda_{1}\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-a\left(\langle h_{1},\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\rangle+\langle h_{2},\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\rangle\right).\end{split}

For the symbol of
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, set
\widehat{-\partial_{1}^{2}}=m_{1}^{2}
,
\widehat{-\partial_{2}^{2}}=m_{2}^{2}
, and
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},k_{2})=(\varepsilon m_{1},\varepsilon^{2}m_{2})
. From (
1.19
):

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-4}\Big(1+\varepsilon^{2}+\sigma|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)|\mathbf{k}|\Big(\tanh{|\mathbf{k}|}-\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon^{-4}\Big(1+\varepsilon^{2}+\sigma|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)|\mathbf{k}|\Big(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{|\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{1}{\frac{3}{|\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{1}{\frac{5}{|\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{7}}}}-\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle>0,

and there exist
C,c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-4}\Big(1+\varepsilon^{2}+\sigma|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)|\mathbf{k}|\Big(\tanh{|\mathbf{k}|}-\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-4}\Big(1+\varepsilon^{2}+\sigma|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\Big)|\mathbf{k}|\Big(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{|\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{1}{\frac{3}{|\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{5}}}-\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{1+\frac{1}{3}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}(m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2})^{3},

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon^{-4}c_{0}|\mathbf{k}|^{5}\quad\text{as}\quad|\mathbf{k}|\to\infty.

This implies

|\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}\phi_{2},\phi_{1}\rangle|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+C\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Using (
4.5
) and Young’s inequality yields

(4.6)

\begin{split}&a^{2}(-\lambda_{1}-\delta_{\varepsilon})\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{32\lambda_{1}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\\
&\quad-\frac{2}{\lambda_{1}}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\frac{2}{\lambda_{1}}\frac{\|\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

where
\delta_{\varepsilon}
depends on
\varepsilon
and can be arbitrarily small as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. In (
4.6
) we have also used
\delta(\varepsilon)\left\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}^{2}
to control the terms
\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}
,
\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla^{4}\phi_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}
, and
\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}^{2}
, due to the fact that
\phi_{1}
is a concrete function. For the same reason, we see that the coefficients before
\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
is specified constant.

Step 4: Energy estimate for
\phi_{2}
.

Multiply (
4.4
) by
\phi_{2}
and use (
4.3
):

(4.7)

\begin{split}&\lambda_{*}\|\nabla\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{*}A\|\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial_{2}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{2}{\lambda_{*}}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{2}{\lambda_{*}}\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2a^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{*}}+\tilde{\delta}_{\varepsilon}\right)\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

Combining (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{a^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{*}}\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\lambda_{*}\|\nabla\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{10}{\lambda_{*}}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left(\frac{8}{\lambda_{*}}\frac{\|\partial_{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}+\frac{2}{\lambda_{*}}\right)\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

implying:

a^{2}\|\nabla\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

For the specific function
\phi_{1}
, we have

\|\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Thus from (
4.7
):

\|\partial_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Consequently

(4.8)

\|\partial_{1}(\partial_{1}q\partial_{1}\phi)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|h_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Step 5: Higher-order estimates via Fourier analysis.

Rewrite (
4.1
) as:

(4.9)

\mathcal{L}_{1}\phi+\mathcal{L}_{2}\phi=\partial_{1}h_{1}+\partial_{2}h_{2}+3\partial_{1}(\partial_{1}q\partial_{1}\phi)=:h.

By (
4.8
) and the definitions of
\|\cdot\|_{b}
,
\|\cdot\|_{c}
:

(4.10)

\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{b}^{2}+\|h_{2}\|_{c}^{2}\right).

The Fourier transform of (
4.9
) is

(4.11)

\widehat{\phi}(m_{1},m_{2})=\frac{\widehat{h}(m_{1},m_{2})}{Am_{1}^{4}+m_{1}^{2}+(1+\varepsilon^{2})m_{2}^{2}+\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}m_{2}^{4}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}.

To bound
\|\phi\|_{a}
, we show each term is controlled by
\|h\|_{L^{2}}
. For
\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\frac{\varepsilon(m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2})^{5/2}\widehat{h}}{Am_{1}^{4}+m_{1}^{2}+(1+\varepsilon^{2})m_{2}^{2}+\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}m_{2}^{4}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\frac{\varepsilon(m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2})^{5/2}}{m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon(m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2})^{5/2}}\widehat{h}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}}.

Other terms in
\|\cdot\|_{a}
follow similarly. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

We would like to emphasize that the use of
tanh
in operator
\mathcal{L}_{2}
employs the method of continued fractions, which is different from previous linearization estimates. Additionally,
\sigma>\frac{1}{3}
can be observed from the expansion of tanh, and
\sigma>\frac{1}{3}
enables operator
\mathcal{L}_{2}
to possess properties similar to those of elliptic operators. This is also the reason why we use
\mathcal{Q}
in the derivation of equations in Section
2
.

After establishing the
L^{2}
theory for equation (
4.1
), we extend the analysis to
L^{p}
spaces for
p\in[4,\infty)
. This extension is crucial for studying the nonlinear problem (
2.10
). We define the following Sobolev spaces:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}:\|f\|_{*}<\infty\right\},\quad\mathcal{F}_{2}:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{e}}:\|f\|_{**}<\infty\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}:\|f\|_{***}<\infty\right\},

equipped with the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{*}

\displaystyle:=\|f\|_{a}+\|f\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\quad p>2,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{**}

\displaystyle:=\|f\|_{b}+\|f\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{***}

\displaystyle:=\|f\|_{c}+\|f\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

The second main result is the following existence and estimate:

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
h_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}
and
h_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{3}
. There exist constants
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the linear equation (
4.1
) admits a solution
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
satisfying

(4.12)

\|\phi\|_{*}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\right).

Moreover,
\phi(x)\to 0
as
|x|\to\infty
.

Proof.

The existence proof follows arguments similar to Proposition 4.2 in
[
47
]
. We thus obtain the estimate

\|\phi\|_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\leq C\big(\|h_{1}\|_{b}+\|h_{2}\|_{c}\big),

where
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is the Hilbert space defined by

\mathcal{E}_{1}:=\big\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}\mid\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}<+\infty\big\},

with norm
\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}^{2}=\langle f,f\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
and inner product

\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big(\partial_{1}^{2}f\,\partial_{1}^{2}g+\nabla f\cdot\nabla g+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{1}\partial_{2}f\,\partial_{1}\partial_{2}g+\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{2}^{2}f\,\partial_{2}^{2}g+(\mathcal{L}_{2}f)g\Big)dxdy.

Clearly,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is a Hilbert space. Moreover,
\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}=0
implies
f=0
: if
\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}=0
, then
f
must be constant, and since
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{ox}}
, we conclude
f=0
.

The key difference in our approach lies in the use of the
L^{p}
-norm, which requires modified norm estimates. We now derive the a-priori estimate (
4.12
) by analyzing
\|\phi\|_{*}
term by term.

First, we estimate
\|\phi\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
. Using the Green’s function representation, we have

\widehat{\phi}(m_{1},m_{2})=\frac{3im_{1}\widehat{\partial_{1}q\,\partial_{1}\phi}+im_{1}\widehat{h_{1}}+im_{2}\widehat{h_{2}}}{Am_{1}^{4}+m_{1}^{2}+(1+\varepsilon^{2})m_{2}^{2}+(2A+\frac{1}{3})\varepsilon^{2}m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}+\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}m_{2}^{4}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}.

Applying the Hardy-Littlewood-Paley theorem on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and the Hausdorff-Young inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\widecheck{\widehat{\phi}}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|\widehat{\widehat{\phi}}(-\xi)\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\phi}\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big\||m|^{-1}\widehat{\partial_{1}q\,\partial_{1}\phi}\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\||m|^{-1}\widehat{h}_{1}\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\||m|^{-1}\widehat{h_{2}}\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big\|\partial_{1}q\,\partial_{1}\phi\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\|h_{1}\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\|h_{2}\big\|_{L^{4/3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\Big).

Next, applying the Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation inequality

\|D^{j}u\|_{L^{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C\|D^{l}u\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\alpha}\|u\|_{L^{p_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{1-\alpha},\quad\frac{1}{p_{1}}=\frac{j}{n}+\left(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{l}{n}\right)\alpha+\frac{1-\alpha}{p_{2}},

we deduce

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{p_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|^{\alpha_{1}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{1-\alpha_{1}}_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|^{\alpha_{2}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{1-\alpha_{2}}_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\Big),

where
\alpha_{1}=\frac{1}{3}(1-\frac{4}{p_{1}})
,
\alpha_{2}=1-\frac{4}{3p_{2}}
, for all
p_{1}\in[4,+\infty)
and
p_{2}\in[\frac{8}{3},+\infty)
.

By the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for
p>2
, it follows that

\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big)

and
\phi(x)\to 0
as
|x|\to\infty
.

Now define
\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)=\phi(x,\varepsilon y)
. Then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big),

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big),\quad m=1,2,3,4,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{5}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\varepsilon^{-3/2}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big).

Using the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we bound the scaled derivatives:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\phi\|_{C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla^{m+2}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big),&m=1,2,\\
C\varepsilon^{-3/2}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{**}+\|h_{2}\|_{***}\big),&m=3.\end{cases}

This completes the proof.
∎

5.
Solving the nonlinear system

This section addresses the solution of the nonlinear systems (
2.4
) and (
2.5
). We seek solutions to (
2.10
) of the form
f=q+\phi
, where
q=q_{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
2.11
). Substituting this ansatz yields

(5.1)

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}\phi=\sum_{i=1}^{4}P_{i}+\frac{3}{2c}\partial_{1}\big((\partial_{1}\phi)^{2}\big)-\mathcal{L}_{2}q-\left(2A+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{1}^{2}\partial_{2}^{2}q-\sigma(1+\varepsilon^{2})\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{2}^{4}q.

From (
2.5
), we express
h
as
h=c(\partial_{1}q+\psi)
, where
\psi
satisfies

(5.2)

\psi-\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})\psi=\partial_{1}\phi+\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})(\partial_{1}q)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}(\partial_{1}q+\partial_{1}\phi)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{c}\Pi.

We solve (
5.1
) in the function space

(5.3)

\mathcal{F}_{\phi}=\left\{\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{1}\mid\|\phi\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon\right\},

with
C
sufficiently large. For
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi}
, the following estimates hold:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon+\varepsilon\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\varepsilon,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{1-\frac{3}{p}}\left(\|q\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\phi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\frac{3}{p}},\quad p\in[4,+\infty].

Furthermore, for
m=1,2,\dots,5
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\xi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{m+1-\frac{3}{2}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{m-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}q\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{m-\frac{1}{2}}.

For
m=1,2,3
,

(5.7)

\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}\leq\varepsilon^{m+1}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}q\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{m+1}.

By the Gagliardo-Nirenberg interpolation inequality, for
m=1,2,3,4
and
p\in[4,+\infty)
,

(5.8)

\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\xi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}\leq C\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m+1}\xi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}^{\theta_{1}}\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m-1}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}^{1-\theta_{1}}\leq C\varepsilon^{m+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}},

where
\theta_{1}=1-\frac{2}{p}\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Next, we consider the linear equation

(5.9)

\psi-\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})\psi=\mathfrak{h}.

Define the Hilbert space
H:=\left\{f\in\mathcal{H}_{e}\mid\|f\|_{h}<+\infty\right\}
with norm

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{h}

\displaystyle:=\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{6}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{5/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon^{7/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

We seek solutions in the space

(5.10)

\mathcal{F}_{\psi}:=\left\{\psi\in H\mid\|\psi\|_{h}\leq C\varepsilon\right\}.

For
\psi\in\mathcal{F}_{\psi}
, we have the estimates:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{m+2-\frac{3}{2}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}h\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{m+\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\partial_{1}q\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{m+\frac{1}{2}},&m=0,1,2,\\
C\varepsilon^{7/2},&m=3,4,5,6.\end{cases}

Similarly,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{m+2}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}(\partial_{1}q)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{2},&m=0,\\
C\varepsilon^{3},&m=1,2,3,4.\end{cases}

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathbf{x^{\prime}}}^{m}\eta\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbf{x^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2+m-\frac{3}{p}}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\partial_{1}q\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\psi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)

(5.13)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{2-\frac{3}{p}},&m=0,\\
C\varepsilon^{3-\frac{3}{p}},&m=1,2,3,4.\end{cases}

We now analyze (
5.9
). For given
\mathfrak{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{5}
, we solve for
\psi\in\mathcal{F}_{\psi}
, where

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}

\displaystyle:=\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\ \quad+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\psi\in H
be a solution of (
5.9
) with
\mathfrak{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{5}
. Then there exist positive constants
\varepsilon_{0}
and
C
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{h}\leq C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.

Proof.

Applying the Fourier transform to (
5.9
) gives

\widehat{\psi}(m_{1},m_{2})=\frac{\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}(m_{1},m_{2})}{1+\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(m_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}m_{2}^{2})}.

From this representation, we derive the high-order derivative estimate:

\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{6}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.

Similar arguments bound the
L^{2}
-norms of lower-order derivatives of
\psi
by
C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}
.

To establish the
L^{\infty}
-bound, define
\overline{\psi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\psi(\varepsilon x_{1},\varepsilon^{2}x_{2})
. This satisfies

\overline{\psi}-\sigma\Delta_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\overline{\psi}=\overline{\mathfrak{h}},

where
\overline{\mathfrak{h}}(x_{1},x_{2})=\mathfrak{h}(\varepsilon x_{1},\varepsilon^{2}x_{2})
. The Green’s function for
-\Delta_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is

G(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}|)=|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}|^{-(n-2)/2}K_{(n-2)/2}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}|)>0,

with
K_{v}(z)
denoting the modified Bessel function of order
v
. Consequently,

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|\overline{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\|\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.

For the weighted Hölder norms, define
\widetilde{\psi}(x,y)=\psi(x,\varepsilon y)
. Then

\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\quad m\geq 0.

The
L^{2}
-norms satisfy

\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}},&m=1,2,3,\\
C\varepsilon^{3-m-1/2}\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}},&m=4,5,6.\end{cases}

By the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we obtain

\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\left(\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla^{m+2}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right).

Combining these estimates yields

\|\nabla^{m}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\begin{cases}C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}},&m=1,\\
C\varepsilon^{1/2-m}\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}},&m=2,3,4.\end{cases}

This completes the proof, as all components of
\|\psi\|_{h}
are bounded by
C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{5}
. Then there exists a solution
\psi\in H
to (
5.9
).
Moreover, for any
\mathfrak{h}_{1},\mathfrak{h}_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{5}
, the corresponding solutions
\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}
and
\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{2}}
satisfy

(5.15)

\left\|\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}-\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{2}}\right\|_{h}\leq C\left\|\mathfrak{h}_{1}-\mathfrak{h}_{2}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.

Proof.

Define the operator
L_{\varepsilon,h}
by

L_{\varepsilon,h}\psi:=\psi-\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})\psi.

Consider the space

\mathcal{E}_{2}:=\left\{f\mid\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}<+\infty\right\},

equipped with the inner product

\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(fg+\sigma\varepsilon^{2}\left(\partial_{1}f\,\partial_{1}g+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}f\,\partial_{2}g\right)\right)dx\,dy

and norm
\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}^{2}=\langle f,f\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}
. Then
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is a Hilbert space, and
\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=0
implies
f=0
. It is straightforward to verify that
L_{\varepsilon,h}
is self-adjoint on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

Define the bilinear form

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}v\,L_{\varepsilon,h}udx\,dy.

This form satisfies

\left|\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v)\right|\leq C\|u\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\|v\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}},

so for fixed
u\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
, the map
v\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v)
is a bounded linear functional on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
. By the Riesz Representation Theorem, there exists a unique
w\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
such that

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v)=\langle w,v\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}},\quad\text{for all }v\in\mathcal{E}_{2}.

Define the operator
A_{h}u=w
, so that

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v)=\langle A_{h}u,v\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}},\quad\text{for all }v\in\mathcal{E}_{2}.

Then
A_{h}:\mathcal{E}_{2}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
is a bounded linear operator.

We show
A_{h}
is injective. Suppose
A_{h}u=0
. Then
\langle A_{h}u,v\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,v)=0
for all
v\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
, implying
L_{\varepsilon,h}u=0
. Using the estimate (
5.14
), this yields
\|u\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=0
, hence
u=0
.

Next, we prove
A_{h}
is surjective. Suppose
w\in R(A_{h})^{\perp}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Then
\langle A_{h}u,w\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=0
for all
u\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which implies
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(u,w)=0
for all
u\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Taking
u=w
gives
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}(w,w)=0
. By the coercivity result in (
5.14
), this implies
\|w\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=0
, so
w=0
. Thus
R(A_{h})=\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

Since
A_{h}
is bijective, for any
\mathfrak{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{5}
, there exists a unique
\psi\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
such that

\langle A_{h}\psi,v\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}v\mathfrak{h}dx\,dy,\quad\text{for all }v\in\mathcal{E}_{2}.

By the definition of
A_{h}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,h}
, this is equivalent to
\psi
satisfying (
5.9
). Furthermore, the boundedness of
A_{h}^{-1}
implies the estimate

\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\leq C\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.

Finally, the linearity of (
5.9
) and (
5.14
) directly yield the Lipschitz estimate (
5.15
).
∎

By (
2.4
), we define

(5.16)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi}&:=\partial_{1}\phi+\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})(\partial_{1}q)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}(\partial_{1}q+\partial_{1}\phi)^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{c}\Pi_{\phi}\\
&\ =\partial_{1}\phi+\sigma\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{2}^{2})(\partial_{1}q)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}f|^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{2c(1+\varepsilon^{6}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2})}\left(-c\partial_{1}h+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{\varepsilon}h\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}f\right)^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{2}}{c}\left(\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[\frac{\nabla_{\varepsilon}h}{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{6}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2}}}-\nabla_{\varepsilon}h\right]\right).\end{split}

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi}
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\psi}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{1}}-\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}+O(\varepsilon)\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}.

Proof.

By the definition of
\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{1}}-\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{2}}

\displaystyle=(\partial_{1}\phi_{1}-\partial_{1}\phi_{2})-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+g_{1}(h_{1})g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}h_{1}-g_{3}(h_{2})\nabla_{\varepsilon}h_{2}\right],

where the coefficient functions are defined as:

\displaystyle g_{1}(h)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{2c(1+\varepsilon^{6}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2})},

\displaystyle g_{2}(h,f)

\displaystyle=\left(-c\partial_{1}h+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla_{\varepsilon}h\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}f\right)^{2},

\displaystyle g_{3}(h)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sigma\varepsilon^{8}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2}}{c\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{6}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2}}\left(1+\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{6}|\nabla_{\varepsilon}h|^{2}}\right)}.

To estimate the difference in
\mathcal{F}_{5}
-norm, we bound each component. First,
by the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}-\partial_{1}\phi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

By the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{*}
, this implies

(5.17)

\|\partial_{1}\phi_{1}-\partial_{1}\phi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}.

Next, consider the second term:

\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})
.
Applying the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}
and Sobolev embedding, we obtain

(5.18)

\begin{split}&\left\|\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\\
&\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C_{3}\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\left[\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right]\right\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

where the Sobolev embedding theorem provides

(5.19)

\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}h\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}h\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\|h\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}h\|_{L^{2}}\right).

The first component in (
5.18
) is bounded by

(5.20)

\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}.

For the second component, we have

(5.21)

\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}.

For the third component, apply the Leibniz rule to the fourth-order gradient:

(5.22)

\begin{split}&\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\left[\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right]\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{4}\binom{4}{k}\left\|\left(\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{k+1}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\right)\cdot\left(\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5-k}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{k+1}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5-k}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{k=3}^{4}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{k+1}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5-k}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

The required derivative norms are bounded as follows:

(5.23)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C,\quad m=4,5,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C,&m=1,2,\\
C\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}},&m=3.\end{cases}

Combining (
5.23
), (
5.24
), and (
5.22
), the dominant term in (
5.22
) is
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
, yielding

(5.25)

\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\left[\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right]\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}.

Synthesizing (
5.20
), (
5.21
), and (
5.25
) into (
5.18
), we conclude

(5.26)

\left\|\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2c}\nabla_{\varepsilon}(2q+\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\leq C\varepsilon\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}.

For the third term in
\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{1}}-\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{2}}
, we decompose the difference as

(5.27)

\begin{split}\|g_{1}(h_{1})g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}&\leq\|g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\\
&\quad+\|\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.\end{split}

Estimation of the first term in (
5.27
).

Applying the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}
and Sobolev embedding (
5.19
),

(5.28)

\begin{split}&\|g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\\
&\leq\|g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C\|g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big[g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\big]\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

We establish the following bounds:

(5.29)

\displaystyle\|g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{4},

(5.30)

\displaystyle\|g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\partial_{1}(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right),

(5.31)

\displaystyle\|g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{4}\left(\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})\|_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\right).

For the high-order derivative term, the Leibniz rule yields:

(5.32)

\begin{split}&\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big[g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\big]\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C\sum_{m=0}^{3}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4-m}\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

The derivatives of
g_{1}
satisfy:

(5.33)

\displaystyle\|g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{4}\quad\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{8},\quad\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{8},

(5.34)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{6},\quad\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}g_{1}(h_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon^{8}.

The difference
g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})
admits the estimates:

(5.35)

\displaystyle\|\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right),

(5.37)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{2}\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right),

(5.38)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{3}\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right),

(5.39)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right).

Combining (
5.33
)–(
5.34
) and (
5.35
)–(
5.39
), we obtain

(5.40)

\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big[g_{1}(h_{1})\big(g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big)\big]\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right).

Estimation of the second term in (
5.27
).

Similarly, we have

(5.41)

\begin{split}&\|\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\\
&\leq\|g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C\|\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big[\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big]\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The uniform bounds yield:

(5.42)

\displaystyle\|g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{9}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h},

(5.43)

\displaystyle\|\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{9}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}.

For the derivative term, Leibniz rule gives

(5.44)

\begin{split}&\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4}\big[\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\big]\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C\sum_{m=0}^{2}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4-m}\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\sum_{m=3}^{4}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{m}g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{4-m}\big(g_{1}(h_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})\big)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon^{6}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}.\end{split}

Synthesis.

Combining (
5.28
)–(
5.31
), (
5.40
), and (
5.42
)–(
5.44
) into (
5.27
), we obtain the estimate:

(5.45)

\|g_{1}(h_{1})g_{2}(h_{1},f_{1})-g_{1}(h_{2})g_{2}(h_{2},f_{2})\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{h}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{*}\right).

Finally, we estimate the last term in
\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{1}}-\mathfrak{h}_{\varepsilon,\phi_{2}}
. We decompose the divergence term as:

(5.46)

\begin{split}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}h_{1}-g_{3}(h_{2})\nabla_{\varepsilon}h_{2}\right]\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}&\leq\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}\\
&\quad+\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[(g_{3}(h_{1})-g_{3}(h_{2}))\nabla_{\varepsilon}h_{2}\right]\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}.\end{split}

Estimation of the first term in (
5.46
).

Applying the
\mathcal{F}_{5}
-norm definition:

(5.47)

\begin{split}\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{\mathcal{F}_{5}}&\leq C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+C\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}^{5}\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The first two components satisfy:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot\left[g_{3}(h_{1})\nabla_{\varepsilon}(h_{1}-h_{2})\right]\|_{L^{\infty}}

(5.48)
